Fred Study Privacy Policy

Beta version, updated 30 October 2026 (version 11)

This is a test (beta) version of the app. Before the app opens to everyone, this policy will go through a legal review and will be updated.

‚Üë ◊í◊®◊°◊î ◊ë◊¢◊ë◊®◊ô◊™ ◊ú◊û◊¢◊ú◊î

Who we are

Fred Study is the beta of a personal study assistant: a phone app that helps Reichman University students organize their course materials from Moodle.

The app is operated by Jonathan Marco, a private individual ("we"), who is responsible for the data described on this page. There is no company at this time. If operation moves to a company, we will update this policy. You can reach us at fredstudent03@gmail.com.

The app does not belong to Reichman University and is not run by it. Moodle is run by the university, and what the university collects about you is covered by its own policy.

The terms of use of the app are on a separate page: fredstudy.github.io/terms.

In short

  • You sign in with Apple or Google. We receive an account ID, your email, your name (if shared), and from Google also a link to your profile picture.
  • The course files your phone downloaded from your Moodle, and the text inside them, are also stored in our cloud in the European Union.
  • If you agree in the app, names and the beginning of files are sent to Google's Gemini service to arrange each course into tabs. Our server does not keep them.
  • If you agree in the app, the text of a lesson or of a file is sent to an OpenAI service to build games and practice questions for you, to summarize lessons and topics for you and to check answers. Summaries and answer checking may go to Anthropic's Claude service instead, when our server chooses it. When picture describing is on (it is off in the first beta version), a slide that is only a picture is sent, as a picture alone, to Anthropic's Claude service (and if it is not available, to Google's Gemini service), to describe it in words. So that Fred can answer you and prepare podcasts, the material and what you wrote to Fred are also sent, to one of the providers for Fred and to both for podcasts. What you write in practice and to Fred is sent to the provider and is not kept on our server, except a report of a mistake in Fred's answer that you confirm yourself, and Fred's memory if you agreed to it (see "Fred, the study chat"). Our server does not keep what is sent, but OpenAI and Anthropic keep it for up to 30 days (see "Artificial intelligence"). We do keep the summary itself on our server, not linked to your account, so that anyone who has exactly the same material of the lesson or topic gets the same summary.
  • If you agree in the app, the text of messages in the track group and of notes on a course page is sent once an hour to an OpenAI or Anthropic service, without names, to write "Fred's notes" for the whole course. This happens only in the community. See "Fred's notes from the community".
  • In the community (the track group and notes on a course page), what you write and your display name are kept on our server, and other students in the group or the course see them. To know which courses you are in, after a full refresh from Moodle your phone sends our server your course list (the ID and name of each course). See "The community".
  • Your Reichman password is never sent to us. If you choose to save it, it stays on your phone only.
  • Automatic refresh is optional, and it is off until you turn it on yourself. If you turn it on, the app may sign in to Reichman for you and download new material, about once every two days and also when you open it. For this, your username and password are stored on the phone without Face ID. They stay on your phone only. See "Automatic refresh".
  • No ads, no tracking, and we do not sell data.
  • You can delete your account from inside the app. Deleting it removes your data in the cloud and on your phone (with a few exceptions, see "Deleting your account").
  • No access to the app? You can ask for deletion by email, see "Deleting your account".

Do you have to give us data?

There is no legal duty to give us data. It is up to you and depends on your consent. In the beta, using the app requires signing in with Apple or Google, and files upload to the cloud. The AI tools (arranging courses, summaries, games and practice questions, answer checking, Fred and podcasts) need your explicit consent: the sign-in screen has two boxes. The first, required: "I have read and agree to the terms of use and the privacy policy", and without the tick you cannot sign in. The second, optional: your consent to sending the material to artificial intelligence, next to a sentence that explains it. You can sign in without ticking the AI consent. Then we ask you the first time a tool needs to send something, and the consent applies to all the tools. You can turn each tool off in your profile. The consent is erased when you sign out and when you delete the account, and you tick again at the next sign-in. A tool that is off, or that you did not agree to, sends nothing. When a tool is off, courses stay in Moodle's order and no games or summaries are built.

What we collect, and why

Account details
When you sign in with Apple or Google, we receive an account ID, an email address and a name, if they were shared. Google also sends a link to your profile picture. If you chose to hide your email with Apple, we receive and store Apple's relay address. The sign-in service also keeps technical details: sign-in time, network (IP) address and device type. All of these are stored at Supabase in Frankfurt.
Why: so you can sign in, and so each student has their own account.
Course files
Every file your phone downloaded from your Moodle is uploaded to our cloud. In the beta, files upload only while the app is open. Files already on your phone upload only on Wi-Fi, and so do new files larger than 10MB. In the cloud we store the file's content, a "fingerprint" of it (a number computed from the content) and its size. The file name and the course are not stored with the file in the cloud. They pass through our server only for the artificial-intelligence tools (arranging courses, games, summaries, Fred and podcasts), and are not kept there. They are sent to the provider when arranging courses, in Fred, and maybe in podcasts; in games and summaries they stay on our server and are not sent to the provider.
Why: to keep a copy of your material for you, and later to open it even when it is no longer on your phone. In the beta, files also stay on your phone.
Text inside the files
Your phone reads the text inside a file and uploads it to the cloud together with the file.
Why: so the text does not have to be read again and again, and for summaries and practice.
File permissions
A record of which files belong to your account: the file's fingerprint and when you got permission to it.
Why: so that only someone who uploaded a file themselves can receive it.
Arranging courses into tabs
See "Artificial intelligence: all the tools".
Games, practice questions, summaries, answer checking, Fred and podcasts
See "Artificial intelligence: all the tools".
The community: the track group and notes on a course page
See "The community".
Operations and security logs
Our server records technical events, such as the type of action, the time, the file's fingerprint and the account ID. For example, when an upload failed, or when a user went over the allowed number of actions. The log contains no file contents. Our cloud provider also keeps a technical log of every request to our server (arranging, games, summaries, reports and more): the account ID, the path, the time and your device's network (IP) address, and at sign-in also the email. This log is kept for 7 days, also after the account is deleted, and then it is deleted automatically.
Why: to run the service, prevent abuse and fix problems.
Crash reports
When the app crashes, and crash reporting is on in your version, a crash report is sent to Sentry, in the European Union: what failed, the phone model, the app version, a random ID of the installation, and a short list of what happened before the crash (screen names and server names). No name, no email and no course material. The IP address is not kept. The reports are deleted after 30 days.
Why: to fix problems.
Testing through TestFlight
If you installed the app through Apple's TestFlight, Apple shares test information with us: device model and iOS version, how many times you used the app, crash reports, and feedback you chose to send. If you were invited by email, also the name and email in the invitation. This information is also covered by Apple's terms.
Why: to fix problems and improve the app.
Testing through Google Play
If you joined the beta through Google Play, we receive the list of the testers' emails and the crash reports from Play Console.
Why: to run the beta, fix problems and improve the app.
Messages to us
If you write to us, we keep your message and our reply for up to a year after the matter is closed.
Why: to answer you and improve the service.

What the app does not have: no ads, no advertising or tracking libraries, and no third-party analytics tools. We do not use your data to track you across other apps or websites.

What stays on your phone and is not sent to us

  • Your Reichman password. If you choose to save them, your username and password are stored only in this phone's secure storage. The app uses them only to fill in the university's sign-in form on your phone. They are not sent to our server or to anywhere else, except Reichman's sign-in page. There are two modes:
    • Without automatic refresh: they are locked behind biometric lock (Face ID on iPhone, fingerprint on Android), and the app uses them only when you press "Refresh".
    • With automatic refresh (only if you turned it on yourself, on a separate screen): they are stored on this phone without Face ID and without a fingerprint, so that the app can sign in even when the phone is locked (after you have unlocked it once since it was switched on). Anyone who unlocks your phone can use them through the app. They are not included in backups and do not move to another device. When you turn automatic refresh off, they are deleted.
    On iPhone, deleting the app does not delete the saved password. For it to be deleted, you first need to press "Turn off and delete" (◊¥◊õ◊ë◊î ◊ï◊û◊ó◊ß◊¥) or "Disconnect account" (◊¥◊†◊™◊ß ◊ó◊©◊ë◊ï◊ü◊¥) in the app. More in the section "Automatic refresh".
  • Your Moodle sign-in. It is kept in the sign-in window inside the app, on your phone. Our code does not send it to the server.
  • Your course list, deadlines, progress and course arrangement. They are stored on your phone. Our server does not keep them, except the course list sent for the community (see "Your course list").
  • A document you shared to Lumo (PDF, Word, slides). It is kept on your phone in the folder of your personal materials, until you delete it. On iPhone the file itself is not included in the device backup (it is kept in the cache folder), only its name and the place you chose for it; on Android the app is not included in the backup. The file itself is not kept on our server, except a file you chose to share with the class pool.
  • The instructions and files of an assignment. The instructions and files of each assignment are read from its page in Moodle and are kept only on your phone. Moodle records that the page was opened, as when you open it yourself.
  • The grade and the lecturer's comments on an assignment you submitted. They are read from the assignment's page in Moodle, in the same visit as the instructions, and are kept only on your phone. They are not sent to our server, to artificial intelligence or to Fred. They are deleted when you press "Disconnect account" (◊¥◊†◊™◊ß ◊ó◊©◊ë◊ï◊ü◊¥).
  • Lecture recordings, forums and the content of online lessons. The app does not take them.

Automatic refresh

Automatic refresh is optional. It is off until you turn it on yourself, on a separate screen in the app. On that screen you read what will happen, type your Reichman username and password, and press "Turn on automatic refresh" (◊¥◊î◊§◊¢◊ú ◊®◊¢◊†◊ï◊ü ◊ê◊ï◊ò◊ï◊û◊ò◊ô◊¥). Agreeing to the terms of use does not turn it on. Only your turning it on, on that screen, does.

  • What it does: the app signs in to Reichman for you and downloads new material from your courses to the phone, without you pressing "Refresh". It may do this in the background, about once every two days, and also when you open the app, if about two days have passed since the last refresh that succeeded. It is the same action as "Refresh": the same pages, at the same pace, and no new kind of request to Reichman.
  • In the background: the phone's system decides when to run it, so there is no promise of a particular time. On iPhone, if you close the app by swiping it up, it updates only when you open it.
  • The password: your username and password are stored only on this phone, without Face ID, so that the app can sign in even when the phone is locked. They are not sent to us or to anywhere else, only to Reichman's sign-in page. Anyone who unlocks your phone can use them through the app. More about the protection: "What stays on your phone and is not sent to us".
  • No notifications: automatic refresh sends no notification at all: not about new material, not about a failure and not about a stop. New material appears in the app when you open it.
  • When the sign-in does not succeed: if the sign-in fails, or Reichman asks for an additional verification, the app stops automatic refresh and does not keep trying. There is also a limit on how many times a day it signs in. The app shows one line on the home screen, and you can press "Refresh" and sign in yourself. If Reichman did not accept the password, automatic refresh turns itself off and the saved password is deleted.
  • Remote stop: we can stop automatic refresh for all users at once, without you updating the app. Every time you open it, the app asks our server whether the stop is on, and keeps on the phone only the answer (yes or no). This request is recorded in the cloud provider's technical log, like any request to our server (see "How long data is kept").
  • How to turn it off: at any time, on the same screen, in the profile. When you turn it off, the password is deleted. It is also deleted when you press "Turn off and delete" (◊¥◊õ◊ë◊î ◊ï◊û◊ó◊ß◊¥) or "Disconnect account" (◊¥◊†◊™◊ß ◊ó◊©◊ë◊ï◊ü◊¥), and when you delete your account.
  • Deleting the app on iPhone: deleting the app from the phone alone does not delete the saved password. For it to be deleted, first press "Turn off and delete" (◊¥◊õ◊ë◊î ◊ï◊û◊ó◊ß◊¥) or "Disconnect account" (◊¥◊†◊™◊ß ◊ó◊©◊ë◊ï◊ü◊¥).

The community: the track group and notes on a course page

In the track group, students of the same track and the same cohort write to each other. On a course page, students of the same course write notes. Other students see what is written there, so it is kept on our server (Supabase in Frankfurt). In the track group you can also send files: documents (PDF, Word (.docx), PowerPoint (.pptx), up to 25MB) and pictures (up to 10MB). For "Fred's notes", which are written from what is said in the community, see "Fred's notes from the community".

Your course list

  • What is sent: after a full refresh from Moodle that succeeded, and only if you chose a track and year in the community, your phone sends our server the full list of your Moodle courses: for each course its ID and its name (up to 60 characters), up to 60 courses, current-semester courses first. A refresh that failed or did not finish sends nothing. Files, and "fingerprints" of files, are not sent.
  • Why: to open for you the notes on the page of each course in the list, and the track group. The server trusts the list the app sends. It does not go into Moodle and cannot check the list itself. The server does not tell your phone who else is in the course.
  • What is kept: which courses you are a member of, when each course last appeared in your list, and the course name you sent. From the names students sent, one name is chosen for each course, by majority. Only Fred (to write "Fred's notes") and whoever reviews reports see this name. Students do not see it.
  • A course that left the list: if a course no longer appears in the list your phone sends, your access to its notes ends after 3 days, and leaving the group follows within an hour. A record that the course was in your list (the course ID and when it last appeared) stays on the server, without access, until you leave the community or delete your account. That way, if the course comes back to the list, the access comes back.
  • How long: the course name you sent is kept until you leave the course, leave the community or delete your account. A record of a course you left yourself stays until your account is deleted, so that the next refresh does not put you back in without you asking.
  • Earlier versions: earlier beta versions also sent "fingerprints" of the course files. Today they are not sent. Fingerprints already sent are deleted when you leave the course, leave the community or delete your account.

What is kept about you in the community

  • Display name: the name you chose for the group and notes (2 to 24 characters). Others see it next to what you wrote. You can change it in settings.
  • Track and year: the track, the starting year of your cohort, and when you last changed track (you can change it up to twice in 30 days).
  • A track you added: if you added a track that was not in the list, we keep its name, when it was added, and that you added it. The name appears at once in the track list for all users. Who added it is not shown to any user. To add a track you first need to accept the community terms.
  • Group membership: which group you are in, when you joined, and if you left: when and why (you left yourself, you changed track, no course is left in your course list, or you were removed).
  • Messages and notes: the text (up to 1,000 characters), when it was written and edited, which message you replied to, and who was tagged in it (up to 10 tags).
  • Files and pictures in the group (when they are on): the file itself is kept on our server, in private storage in the European Union (Frankfurt), together with the message it belongs to. All members of the same group can open it. Fred does not read pictures. Fred sorts a document (PDF, Word, PowerPoint) only when sorting group documents is on and only if the sender agreed: then a short, filtered piece of text leaves the document, not the file itself (see "Sorting documents from the group"). Only text is sent for "Fred's notes". Each message has at most one file. For each picture, a small preview picture is also kept. With each file we also keep its name (up to 120 characters), its size and its type, and for a picture also its width and height. Group members see the name next to the file. The name is not used in the file's address on the server, or in the name of the file that is downloaded. We also keep a "fingerprint" of the file: a number computed from its content. It is used only to check the file (that it was not replaced, and that the picture that was checked is the same picture). It does not leave the server, and it is not used to compare users. A file you uploaded and did not attach to a message is deleted after about 2 hours. Before sending, the phone removes the location and other hidden details inside pictures, and the server rejects a picture that still has such details. Every picture is checked automatically for offensive content before other members see it. Until the check passes, only the sender sees the picture, and a picture that fails is deleted from our server right away. Someone who did not agree to sending to artificial intelligence can send documents, but not pictures (see "Checking pictures in the track group"). Documents are sent as they are, including the author name written inside the file. The file is kept until the sender deletes it (together with its message), until the account is deleted, or until the group itself is deleted. One exception: a file of a reported message is kept for the reviewer like the copy of the text in the report: until the decision, and at most 30 days, even if the writer deleted the message or the account. When a group is deleted, all its files are deleted, also those of a reported message. A file that a group member opened or saved stays on their device, and our deletion does not reach that copy.
  • Invite link: if you created an invite link to the group, we keep a "fingerprint" of the link (not the link itself), the group, who created it and when. The link is valid for 14 days. Whoever opens it sees the group's track and year. The link does not let into the group anyone who did not choose that track and year, or who has no course in their course list.
  • Blocks: whom you blocked, as a random ID and not as a name.
  • Reports you sent: see below.
  • Terms of use and consent to AI: which version of the terms of use you accepted and when, which version of the consent to AI you gave and when, and when you withdrew it, if you did. The server checks this before you can write in the community, and before a message of yours is sent for "Fred's notes". This record on the server is kept until your account is deleted, also after you sign out.
  • Rate limits: a short record of when you wrote, reported or created an invite, to limit amounts. Deleted after 2 days.

Who sees what

  • The track group: only current members of the same group see the messages, with the writer's display name. The group has everyone who chose that track and year in the app and has at least one course in their course list. The group opens even when it has only one member. Someone who joins later also sees messages written before they joined. Whoever left, was removed or changed track stops receiving messages.
  • Notes on a course page: only students who have the course in their course list see them.
  • Tracks students added: the name of a track someone added appears at once for all users, in the track list. Who added it is not shown.
  • Files in the group: all members of the same group, and only they, see and open them. A picture is shown to others only after it passed the check. A document also shows the author name written inside the file, if there is one.
  • What others do not see: your email, the name from your account and your Moodle name. There is no list of all the group's members. Tagging (@) shows only people who wrote in the last 200 messages.
  • Number of members: the group shows how many members it has. In a small group (for example in a master's degree), this number together with the group's name can hint at who is in it.

Filtering, reports, blocks and removal

  • Filtering: before a display name, message or note is saved, it is checked against a list of words. A message that looks like a password is not stored and not sent to the group.
  • Reports: you can report someone else's message or note. Usually the item is hidden right away for everyone except its writer: for the writer it looks normal, and they do not know it was reported. The server keeps: who reported, what, the reason you chose, what you wrote in the details (up to 300 characters), and a copy of the text as it was at the moment of the report.
  • Who reviews: We (the operator) review the reports. We see the copy, where it was written, the reason and details, the writer's display name, and how many earlier reports against them were accepted. We do not see who reported, only how many of that reporter's earlier reports were reversed, without identity. Whoever was reported does not know who reported them. The decision: restore the item, keep it hidden, or remove the writer.
  • How long: the copy is deleted at the decision, or after 30 days if there is no decision yet, even if the writer deleted the message or their account. The report itself is kept until the decision, and deleted 90 days after it. An item that was kept hidden is deleted 30 days after the decision.
  • Blocks: messages and notes of someone you blocked are not shown to you anywhere. Only you see whom you blocked. The blocked person is not told. If they tag you, the tag is kept as usual, and only the notification to you is not sent, so that they do not find out they were blocked. A block is kept until you remove it (in the "Blocked" list), or until either of you deletes their account.
  • Removal: someone removed from the community leaves the group, cannot read or write in the group and notes, and everything they wrote, including the files and pictures they sent, is hidden and deleted 30 days after the decision. What they shared in the class library stays (see "The class library"). The server keeps the fact that they were removed and when, until their account is deleted.

Leaving, and what stays

  • Messages and notes are kept until you delete them or your account. The end of the semester does not delete them, and neither does leaving the group or the community. When you delete a message, its text is deleted and it disappears for everyone. Other people's replies to it are shown without the quote.
  • Leaving the group: you stop receiving messages. Your track, year and course list stay. The record that you left is kept until your account is deleted.
  • Leaving the whole community: your track and year are deleted, and so are the course names you sent (and old fingerprints, if any were sent). Your course memberships end, so the course notes close for you too. The server keeps only a mark that you left the community, so that we do not store your course list again until you return to the community. What you shared in the class library stays visible to the course's students, with your display name, until you remove it or delete your account.
  • Technical records: changes to your access, and events that tell phones to remove a message that was deleted or hidden: 7 days. Invite links: 7 days after they expire.
  • Deleting your account: see "Deleting your account".

The class library (when it is on)

In the class library, students of the same course share Lumo summaries and their own files with each other. Sharing is always your choice: nothing enters the library unless you tap "Share".

  • Who sees it: only students who have the course in their course list (see "Your course list"). They see what you shared and your display name. They do not see your email, the name on your account or your Moodle name.
  • Sharing a Lumo summary: the phone sends only identifiers: the course id, "fingerprints" of the lesson's files, the ids of the summary's parts, the lesson id, and, if the summary covers only part of the files, the page or slide numbers. The course name, the file names and the lesson name are not sent. The server copies the text from the shared summary we keep (see "How long data is kept"), checks it against a word list, and keeps a copy linked to you in the library. Someone who has the same files sees the lesson name; others see "A summary of a lesson from the course".
  • Sharing a file: PDF, Word (.docx) or PowerPoint (.pptx), up to 25MB. What is sent: the file itself, its "fingerprint", its type, the course id, a title (2 to 80 characters; it is the file name unless you changed it, and when sharing from your own material or from WhatsApp it is always the file name), a description if you wrote one (up to 300 characters), the lesson if you chose one, and the size. The file is shared as it is, including a name written inside it or in the file's details. Before sharing you confirm that this is your own work or that you are allowed to share it, and that it is not the lecturer's material (see the terms of use). A file identical to a Moodle course file that your phone already uploaded to the cloud is refused. The title and description are checked against a word list. The file's content is not checked automatically; the server only checks that it really is a PDF, Word or PowerPoint file, without macros. The file is not sent to AI.
  • Where: the files and the summary copies are kept on our server (Supabase in Frankfurt), in private storage. There is no open address: a file opens only through a temporary link that the server creates only for a member of the course. A file's "fingerprint" is never given to someone who does not have the file.
  • Limits: up to 30 files and 300MB per student, and up to 20 shares a day.
  • Notes: you can write notes on an item in the library, like the course notes. They are deleted with the item.
  • Removing: you can remove any item you shared, at any time, from the library or from "What I shared" in your profile, also after you left the course. The item and its notes are deleted for everyone in Lumo, and the file is deleted from storage. Someone who already opened or saved the file on their device keeps that copy; our deletion does not reach it.
  • Reports and blocks: you can report an item, also for copyright. Usually it is hidden at once from everyone except the person who shared it. A copy for the reviewer (the file with its title and description, or the summary text) is kept until the decision, and at most 30 days, even if the item was removed or the account deleted. An item that is decided to stay hidden is deleted 30 days after the decision. A block hides from you every item of the person you blocked. A lecturer, the university or a rights holder can ask for removal at fredstudent03@gmail.com.
  • Removal of a user: when someone is removed from the course or the community, the items they shared in the library stay, except an item that was reported and decided to stay hidden.
  • How long: an item is kept until it is removed, until the team deletes the course, or until the account is deleted. An upload that did not finish is deleted within a few hours. What is in the database (the summary text, the title, the description and the notes) is also in the database backup for up to 7 days. Files are not in that backup.

Identical files from several students

  • One copy of each identical file. If several students upload exactly the same file, one copy is stored in the cloud.
  • Only someone who uploaded a file themselves gets permission to it, after their own phone downloaded it from their own Moodle.
  • No one sees another user's files, and you cannot learn through the app who else holds a file. The server is built so that its answer does not reveal whether the file was already in the cloud.
  • Files are stored privately. There is no open list of files and no address that can be guessed. A file is opened only through a temporary link, which the server creates only for someone who has permission.
  • If a lecturer removes, hides or replaces a file in Moodle after you uploaded it, the copy you uploaded stays yours. A student who had not uploaded it before will not receive it. A lecturer or the university who want us to remove a file can contact us at fredstudent03@gmail.com.
  • A file that no longer has any permission is deleted from the cloud, together with its text (see "Deleting your account").

Artificial intelligence: all the tools

Several things in the app use artificial intelligence from three providers, Google, OpenAI and Anthropic: arranging courses into tabs, summarizing lessons and topics (including describing pictures in slides with Anthropic's Claude, or with Google's Gemini when it is not available, when describing is on), building games and practice questions and checking answers (summaries and answer checking with OpenAI, or with Anthropic when our server chooses it), talking to Fred (the study chat), preparing podcasts, "Fred's notes" from the community (OpenAI or Anthropic, only in the community), arranging documents from the group into your own material (Google's Gemini, when it is on), and checking pictures sent in the track group (Google's Gemini). Videos will be added later, on the same principle: course material is sent to Google and OpenAI. Before such a tool opens, we will update this page with exactly what is sent. Each tool happens only after you agree: in the second box on the sign-in screen (optional), or the first time a tool needs to send something, if you did not tick it (the consent applies to all the tools). You can sign in without ticking it, and you can turn each tool off in your profile. The sentence shown next to the second box: "Fred Study sends the course materials and what you write in it, through our server, to Google, OpenAI and Anthropic, in order to arrange the courses, summarize, write questions and check answers, answer your questions (for this Fred also receives your list of courses, your deadlines and your progress in the app), and prepare podcasts and videos. A slide or page that is only a picture is sent as a picture to Anthropic or Google, to describe it in words in the summary. What you write in the group and in the notes on a course page is sent without names to OpenAI or Anthropic, to write ‚ÄúFred's notes‚Äù for the whole course. A file you send in the group is sent to Google or Anthropic, so Fred can arrange it in your material and in the material of group members who save it, and a file you share with the class is also used by your classmates' Fred, to answer them and help them study. A picture you send in the group is checked automatically by Google for offensive content before others see it. Fred remembers you, no need to explain everything again each time :) (he keeps things you told him and sends them with your questions to Fred)" The profile also has three separate switches: "Fred's notes from the group", "Sending pictures in the group" and "Arranging files from the group". Each tool has its own details.

Artificial intelligence can be wrong. Summaries, questions, answer checking, Fred's answers and "Fred's notes" are written by artificial intelligence, and they can contain mistakes. It is worth checking factual claims in the answers yourself, against the course material.

What you write in the app is sent in only three cases: the questions you ask Fred (and with them Fred's memory, if you agreed to it), the free answers you write in practice, and the messages and notes you write in the community (for "Fred's notes", see below). In all the other tools nothing you wrote is sent. Pictures you send in the track group are sent for a check (see "Checking pictures in the track group").

Arranging courses (Google Gemini)

The app arranges each course into tabs, based on the Moodle sections and the content of the files. This happens only after you agree to artificial intelligence (in the second box at sign-in, or the first time a tool needs to send something; the consent applies to all the tools), and you can turn it off in your profile. To do this:

  • What is sent: the names of the course, sections, folders and files as they appear in Moodle (for example a tutor's name, when a folder is named after them), the headings and the text the lecturer wrote in a section and next to a file (up to 1,500 characters per section and 500 next to a file; cleaned of emails, links (only the site name stays), phone numbers and runs of 9 digits (like an ID number)), the site name of an external link (not the full address), the group name when a file is limited to a group, and the beginning of the text of each file: up to 600 characters from the first page and up to 300 from the second. On refresh, a summary of the current arrangement is also sent: names, places, and the year, semester and sitting (moed) of exams that were recognized, no file text. Not sent: the full file, your name or email, your password, which tutor is yours, forums or grades.
  • Where it goes: your phone sends this to our server, and our server sends it to Google's Gemini service, on the paid tier. The answer, that is the arrangement, goes back to your phone and is stored there.
  • What is kept: the content passes through our server only while the request is processed, and is not saved. Our log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the cost, the model and counts, never the content. Under Google's terms for the paid service, Google does not use the requests and responses to improve its products. It logs them for a limited period, to detect and prevent prohibited use and for legally required disclosures (Google's usage-policies page states 55 days).
  • Where: Google may process this information outside the European Union and outside Israel.

Games and practice questions (OpenAI)

For each lesson the app builds questions, term-and-definition pairs and harder questions, and checks them against the lesson's text. To do this:

  • What is sent: the full text of every unit of the lesson (slides and the speaker notes in the slides, or pages of a course file, not only the beginnings as when arranging courses), cleaned of emails, links (only the site name stays), phone numbers and runs of 9 digits (like an ID number). Also sent are the items already built for the lesson (questions, answers and explanations that the AI wrote), and, when they are checked, the text of the units they cite. When more items that suit you are built, only a plan is sent: which topic to write about, and whether as a question from a new angle or a harder question. Your numbers (how many times you saw a topic, how many times you were right or wrong) and grades are not sent to the provider; our server uses them only to build the plan. Not sent to OpenAI: the course name, the lesson name, the file name, your name or email, your account ID, and anything you wrote in the app. Your phone sends the course, lesson and file names to our server so it can put them on the games that come back to you; the server does not pass them to the provider and does not keep them.
  • Where it goes: your phone sends this to our server, and our server sends it to an OpenAI service, through its API (the interface programs use to talk to the service) and with the setting not to store the conversation (store: false). The games and their links to the source (the quotes from the units) come back to your phone and are stored there.
  • What is kept: the content passes through our server only while the request is processed, and is not saved. Our log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the cost, the model and counts, never the content. OpenAI keeps requests and responses for up to 30 days to detect and prevent abuse, unless the law requires longer, or it is reasonably necessary to protect its services or others from harm. This applies even though we ask it not to store the conversation (store: false), because we have no Zero Data Retention agreement with OpenAI. Under OpenAI's API terms, data sent to it is not used to train or improve its models unless the customer opts in, and we have not.
  • Where: OpenAI may process this information in the United States and other places outside the European Union and outside Israel. We have no regional processing in Europe.

Sorting documents from the group (Google's Gemini)

A document (PDF, Word, PowerPoint) sent in the track group can be sorted into your own material: a course and a place are chosen for it. This happens only when the feature is on, only if the sender agreed to artificial intelligence in the current version and turned on the "Arranging files from the group" switch in the profile, and only if whoever asks for the sorting has consent too. Pictures and voice recordings are not sorted this way. To do this:

  • What is sent: the phone of the sender, or of a group member who saves the file, extracts a short piece of text from the document (up to about 1,500 characters) and filters it. The piece, the file name and its format, and the names of the courses and places that the requester has in their own material, are sent. The file itself is not sent, and the file ID is not sent to the provider. A document found to contain a secret, such as a password, is not sent for sorting.
  • Where it goes: the phone sends to our server, and our server sends to Google's Gemini service, on the paid tier, as in arranging courses. The chosen course and place go back to the phone.
  • What is kept: our server checks the sender's consent every time, does not open the file and does not keep the text, not even in the log. The log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the cost, the model and counts. At Google, what is written in Arranging courses applies.
  • Without consent: the document is sent in the chat as usual and saved without sorting. For a member who saved it, their Fred does not use it either. A member can always download the file and add it again as their own file; then their Fred uses it under that member's own consent.
  • Where: Google may process this information outside the European Union and outside Israel.

Summaries (OpenAI or Anthropic, and describing pictures with Anthropic or Google)

The app summarizes a lesson or a topic into points, following the course arrangement: all the files of the unit together, not one file. Next to each point there is a link to the slide or page it came from. Exams and practice sheets are not summarized. A summary is made automatically for every new lesson or topic, as long as the tool is on in your profile. To do this:

  • What is sent: the text of the units of all the files of the lesson or topic (slides and the speaker notes in the slides, or pages of a course file, in order; not slides the lecturer hid). The text is cleaned of emails, links (only the site name stays), phone numbers and runs of 9 digits (like an ID number). When picture describing is on, a slide or page that is only a picture is sent first, as a picture alone, to Anthropic's Claude service, and if it is not available to Google's Gemini service (see "Describing pictures" below), and the description that comes back goes into the summary request. For pictures and charts that were not described (for example a picture on a slide that also has text, or when the description failed), a note is sent (for example "this unit has 2 pictures that were not read"). Long material is sent in several parts. Not sent to OpenAI or Anthropic: the course name, the lesson name and the file name, the course and file IDs, your name or email, your account ID, grades, and anything you wrote in the app.
  • Where it goes: your phone sends this to our server, and our server sends it to an OpenAI service, through its API and with the setting not to store the conversation (store: false), or to Anthropic's Claude service, through its API, when our server chooses it (our server decides). The summary comes back to your phone and is stored there.
  • What is kept: the request passes through our server only while it is processed, and is not saved. The summary that comes back is saved on our server as a shared summary (see the next point). Our log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the cost, the model and counts, never the content. At OpenAI the same applies as for games: it keeps requests and responses for up to 30 days, we have no Zero Data Retention agreement, and the data is not used for training (see above). When the summary is sent to Anthropic, what is written in the "Anthropic (Claude)" section applies.
  • Where: OpenAI and Anthropic may process this information in the United States and other places outside the European Union and outside Israel.
  • Describing pictures (Anthropic's Claude, or Google Gemini when it is not available), when this feature is on (it is off in the first beta version): What is sent: The provider receives one picture per request (JPEG, PNG or WebP, up to 1.2MB), with a fixed instruction to describe it, and nothing else: no course, lesson or file name, no IDs and no account. A picture cannot be cleaned like text: an email, a phone number or a photo of a person that appears in the picture reaches the provider as it is. (What Anthropic keeps: see "Anthropic (Claude)".) The cleaning (emails, links, phone numbers and runs of 9 digits) runs only on the description that comes back. Where it goes: your phone sends to our server, and our server sends to Anthropic's Claude (today Haiku). Only when Anthropic is not available does our server send to a Google Gemini model (today Flash-Lite), with the same paid key as arranging courses. What is kept: the picture is not kept by us because of the description, and the description is not kept on the phone. The description, as text only, is kept with the shared summary, not linked to any account, by a fingerprint of the picture, up to 180 days from its last use; anyone who does not have the same picture does not receive it. Our log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the picture size, the cost, the model and counts, never the picture or the description. At Anthropic, what is written in the section "Anthropic (Claude)" applies, and at Google, what is written in the section on arranging courses. There is no separate button: this is part of the summary, controlled by the consent to artificial intelligence and by turning the summaries off in the profile.
  • Shared summary: whoever has exactly the same material of the lesson or topic gets the same summary. The summary is saved on our server not linked to any account, by a fingerprint (a number computed from the text that was sent), with no student ID and without the material itself (except picture descriptions, which are text describing the slide and are kept by a fingerprint of the picture; see "Describing pictures"). It is kept up to 180 days from its last use. This way only someone who has the material itself receives it. Deleting an account does not delete a shared summary, because it holds nothing that relates to you. After two reports of a mistake, the summary is deleted and written again.
  • "Report a mistake": next to each point of a summary there is such a button. When you tap it, our server adds one to a count of reports by day, type, model and prompt version, without the account ID, the point, the text of the summary or the file name. The count itself has no account ID: we do not keep who tapped. The request itself is recorded in the cloud provider's technical log, like every request to the server (see "How long data is kept"). The count is not sent to the AI provider. After two reports of a mistake, the summary is deleted and written again. The beta has no second model checking the summary, and this button is how we learn about mistakes.

Answer checking in practice (OpenAI or Anthropic)

When you write a free answer to a question in practice, the app checks it against the material. To do this:

  • What is sent: the question, its expected answer and key points, one to three units of the material that the question cites (a slide, page or passage), and the answer you wrote. The material is cleaned of emails, links (only the site name stays), phone numbers and runs of 9 digits. The answer you wrote is cleaned only of emails and links: if you wrote a phone number or an ID number in it, they are sent as they are, so do not write such things in an answer. Not sent: the course name, the file name, your name or email, your account ID, speaker notes and grades.
  • Where it goes: your phone sends this to our server, and our server sends it to an OpenAI service, through its API and with the setting not to store the conversation (store: false), or to Anthropic's Claude service, through its API, when our server chooses it (our server decides). The verdict and the feedback come back to your phone.
  • What is kept: the request, the answer you wrote and the verdict pass through our server only while the request is processed, and are not saved. Our log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the cost, the model and counts, never the content. At OpenAI the same applies as for games: it keeps requests and responses for up to 30 days, we have no Zero Data Retention agreement, and the data is not used for training (see above). When the check is sent to Anthropic, what is written in the "Anthropic (Claude)" section applies.
  • Where: OpenAI and Anthropic may process this information in the United States and other places outside the European Union and outside Israel.
  • "I think I was right": when you tap it, our server adds one to a count by day, type, model and prompt version. The count itself has no account ID: we do not keep who tapped. The request itself is recorded in the cloud provider's technical log, like every request to the server (see "How long data is kept"). The count is kept without a time limit.

Fred, the study chat (Google or OpenAI)

Fred answers questions about a course or a lesson, based on the material. The provider is one of the two, Google (Gemini) or OpenAI, and the choice is set on the server. The details of each provider (how long it keeps data, where it processes) are written above, in the sections on Gemini and OpenAI, and apply here too. To do this:

  • What is sent: what you wrote to Fred, that is your question and up to the last six questions and answers of the conversation; the type of conversation (about the material or brainstorming); the course name; the lesson name when the question is about a lesson (and for a question about a course, also the list of lesson names); the file names; and passages of the material: a whole lesson, or about twenty units related to the question plus their neighbors. The file name is attached to each passage. When the question is about a deadline of an assignment or an exam, the title and the date of that item from the calendar are sent too. Everything, including what you wrote, is cleaned of emails, links, phone numbers and runs of 9 digits. Not sent: speaker notes, slides the lecturer hid, identifying details, cookies, file addresses, Moodle IDs, your name, grades and forums.
  • What Fred knows about your semester: when you ask Fred, the AI provider is also sent the list of your courses and lessons, the file names, your deadlines and your progress in the app, so that Fred can answer questions about the semester. Not sent: grades from Moodle (a grade you added yourself to Fred's memory is sent, see below), group messages or names of other people.
  • Fred's memory (only after you tick the consent whose wording mentions it): Fred remembers things you added yourself on the "What Fred remembers about me" screen, for example a quiz grade, extra time in exams or a busy week, so you do not have to explain everything again each time. You can see, correct, delete and turn off all of it on that screen. If you did not tick the updated consent, Fred works as before, without memory. Coming soon, not on yet: Fred remembering by himself things you told him in your private conversation, and asking you in the conversation "Remember this?". Until then an item enters the memory only when you add it yourself on the screen. What is sent: (1) (coming soon, not on yet) after an answer of Fred, your message and up to one earlier message of yours (up to 2,000 characters each), and the current memory list, are sent through our server to an OpenAI service with the setting not to store the conversation (store: false), to find what is worth remembering. When the link to assignments is on, the names of your courses and the id of the chat's course are sent with them too, to recognise a task the lecturer gave in a course (for example "the lecturer said to submit by Sunday"). Our server does not keep this task; it goes back to your phone. Not sent: Fred's answers, course material, group messages and content you shared into the app from another app. (2) The memory (up to 30 items and up to 3,000 characters) is sent with each question you ask Fred, to the provider that answers it. What is kept: the items are kept on our server, linked to your account, so they are on all your devices. Only you see them: not in groups, not in "Fred's notes" and not by other users, and they are not used for advertising or tracking. A temporary thing ("I'm busy this week") is deleted after 7 days. An item you delete is deleted at once, and only a marker without the content stays up to 30 days, so your other devices know. Everything is deleted when you delete the account; "Disconnect" deletes only the copy on the phone. Passwords, codes, card numbers and ID numbers are never kept. Information about family, health or money is kept only when you asked for it explicitly or answered "Yes" when asked "Remember this?" (today it appears when you add such an item on the screen). Our server does not write the memory's content to its log.
  • Where it goes: your phone sends this to our server, and our server sends it to the provider's service, through its API. The answer comes back to your phone.
  • What is kept: the conversation history is kept on your phone and not on our server, and is deleted when you disconnect or delete the account. Our server does not keep a question, an answer or a history. One exception: under every answer of Fred there is a "Report a mistake" button. When you tap it you see what will be sent (the question, the answer with its links, and words of your own if you want). Only if you confirm, the question, the answer and your words are kept on our server for up to 90 days (deleted by a daily cleaning) and are deleted with the account. The text is cleaned before it is saved of emails, links, phone numbers and runs of 9 digits. We read the reports in order to fix Fred. Our log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the cost, the model and counts, never the content. At the provider: as written above.
  • Understanding the question: before Fred answers, a short separate call works out what the question is about (for example a deadline, material, or help with the app). It is sent the question you wrote (cleaned of emails, links, phone numbers and runs of 9 digits), up to the last 6 messages of this conversation (yours and Fred's, each up to 600 characters, cleaned the same way, so a follow-up like "explain that again" is understood), the names of your courses and lessons, the state of the semester, the course you opened Fred from if any, and whether there was already a conversation about the material. Not sent: course material, deadlines, grades and text from earlier conversations. It goes through our server to an OpenAI service (not Anthropic), with the setting not to store the conversation (store: false), even when Fred's answer itself comes from Google. Our server does not keep the question or these messages and does not log them (the log records only the account ID, the time, the cost, the model and counts). At OpenAI the same things apply as for its other tools (see "Games and practice questions").

Podcasts (OpenAI writes, Google reads aloud)

The app prepares from a course file an audio episode with two voices, in several parts. To do this:

  • What is sent: to our server are sent the course name, the lesson name and the file name, all the units of the file (without speaker notes), the summary of the lesson or topic the file belongs to, and, in the requests for the next parts, also the outline and the script already written, and a random number that identifies the episode. The text is cleaned of emails, links, phone numbers and runs of 9 digits. Our server sends the material to two providers: the writer (OpenAI) and the script checker (Google), and the course, lesson and file names may be sent to them together with the material. For reading aloud, to Google's Gemini TTS service, only the approved script is sent, without the course material. Not sent: identifying details, cookies, file addresses, the Moodle course number, your name, grades and forums.
  • What is kept: when the service is on, our server prepares the episode even when the app is closed, so it keeps temporarily what the job needs and what was produced along the way (the course material that was sent, outline, script and audio parts): up to 24 hours from the start of the job, and up to 7 more days in the database backup. The input is deleted earlier, when the job finishes or fails. A finished episode is kept in your personal library in the cloud, which only you can read, until you delete it or the account, together with its title (for example the lesson name) and date. A copy is also kept on your phone until you delete it, and it is deleted when you disconnect or delete the account. Our log records only the account ID, the random episode ID, the material tag (see "How long data is kept"), the time, the type of action, the cost, the model and counts, never the content.
  • Sharing: you can share an episode only when it is complete, through your phone's share option. There is no episode shared across the class.
  • At the providers: OpenAI keeps data for up to 30 days, and Google for a limited period, as written above.

Checking pictures in the track group (Google's Gemini)

Every picture sent in the track group is checked automatically for offensive content, before other members of the group see it. To do this:

  • What is sent: only the picture (and its small preview picture, which is checked too), after the phone removed its location and other hidden details. The account ID, the message text and the file name are not sent.
  • Where to: our server sends it to Google's Gemini service, in the paid version. Until the check passes, only the sender sees the picture. A picture that fails the check is deleted from our server right away and not kept. Only the sender knows: they still see it from the copy on their own phone, marked "not published".
  • Notice in advance: before the first picture you send, the app shows: "The picture will be checked automatically by Google before others see it."
  • A condition for sending pictures: the check is a condition for sending pictures in the group. Someone who did not agree to sending to artificial intelligence can send documents in the group, but not pictures.
  • A picture not checked in time: a picture whose check did not finish within 1 hour is marked "not checked". It is deleted from our server like a blocked picture, and others do not see it.
  • What we keep: the result of the check (passed or blocked), without any description of the picture. When a picture is blocked, we also keep the reason, from a fixed list: sexual content, violence, hate, self-harm, other, or "not checked". Among group members, only the sender sees the reason, next to their message. The result and the reason are kept until the message is deleted. When a picture is blocked because of its content, we also record who sent it, when, and the reason. This record is kept 30 days. If several pictures of the same person are blocked within 30 days, the reviewer gets an alert, and sees how many pictures were blocked and for which reasons. The user is not blocked automatically. The picture itself is kept like any file in the group (see "What is kept about you in the community").
  • At Google: as with arranging courses (see "Arranging courses"): Google does not use the requests to improve its products, and logs them for a limited period.
  • Where: Google may process the data outside the European Union and outside Israel.

Anthropic (Claude)

Four tools can send data to Anthropic's Claude service, all only after you agreed to artificial intelligence in the consent version that names Anthropic:

  • What is sent: (1) Describing pictures (see "Summaries" above): a slide or page that is only a picture, as a picture alone (up to 1.2MB), with no course, lesson or file name, no IDs and no account. A picture cannot be cleaned: an email, a phone number or a photo of a person that appears in the picture reaches Anthropic as it is. (2) "Fred's notes" from the community, when our server chooses Anthropic: the same cleaned text without names described in the notes section. (3) Summaries, when our server chooses Anthropic: the same cleaned text of the lesson or topic, with no course, lesson or file name, described in the summaries section. (4) Answer checking in practice, when our server chooses Anthropic: the question, the expected answer, the units of material and the answer you wrote, as described in the answer checking section.
  • Where to: your phone sends to our server, and our server sends to Anthropic's service, through its API. The answer comes back to our server.
  • What is kept: our server does not keep what is sent to Anthropic. Our log records only the account ID, the time, the type of action and whether it succeeded, the cost, the model and counts, never the content. According to Anthropic, it deletes requests and answers within 30 days. A request its systems flagged as violating its usage policy can be kept up to 2 years, and longer when the law requires it. We have no "zero data retention" agreement with Anthropic. According to Anthropic, by default it does not use requests and answers from its commercial products (such as the API) to train its models.
  • Training: under Anthropic's API terms, the data is not used to train its models without express permission. We do not give such permission.
  • Where: Anthropic may process the data in the United States and other places outside the European Union and outside Israel.

"Fred's notes" from the community (OpenAI or Anthropic)

In the track group and in the notes students write on a course page, people say things the whole course needs to know, for example that a quiz was moved. Once an hour our server reads what was written and turns it into "Fred's notes": short facts, without names and without personal information. This happens only in the community (the track group and notes on a course page), and nowhere else in the app. To do this:

  • What is sent: the text of new messages in the track group and of new notes on a course page (text only, no pictures or files), the course names, and Fred's existing notes for those courses. Before sending, our server removes links, emails, phone numbers and hidden characters from the text, replaces names of group members with [name], and puts a temporary alias (s1, s2) instead of the sender. A message that looks like a password or a secret code is not sent at all. Not sent: senders' names, account IDs, Moodle IDs, and messages that were deleted or hidden.
  • Whose: only messages and notes of people who agreed to sending to artificial intelligence, in the consent version that includes "Fred's notes". If someone did not agree, or withdrew, their messages are not sent.
  • Where to: our server sends this to an OpenAI or an Anthropic service (our server decides). To OpenAI: through its API and with the setting not to store the conversation (store: false), like the other OpenAI tools. To Anthropic: see "Anthropic (Claude)". The answer comes back to our server and is checked there before it is kept.
  • What we keep: only Fred's notes that passed the checks, and a reference to the messages they were written from. What is sent to OpenAI and what comes back are not kept. Our log records only numbers (time, count, cost and result), never content. The notes are kept until 30 days after the end of the semester (if the end date of the semester is not known, the semester is taken to end 12 months after the course was created with us). A note whose source messages were all deleted is hidden at once.
  • At OpenAI: as with the other OpenAI tools (see "Games and practice questions"): OpenAI keeps requests and answers for up to 30 days, to detect and prevent abuse, unless the law requires keeping them longer. This is true also with store: false, because we have no "zero data retention" agreement with OpenAI. Under OpenAI's API terms, the data is not used to train its models. The messages themselves stay in the community like any message; "not kept" refers to the copy sent to OpenAI and its answer.
  • Where: OpenAI may process the data in the United States and other places outside the European Union and outside Israel.
  • Course name: the course name comes from the course list the app sends our server after a full refresh, when you chose a track and year (see "Your course list"), so the notes appear under the right name.

Who we share data with

We do not sell data, do not share it for advertising, and do not pass it to data brokers. We use service providers that process data only on our behalf, to run the app:

  • Supabase: the database, account sign-in, storage of files and text, and server functions. Supabase also keeps a technical log of every request to the server (account ID, path, time and IP address, and at sign-in also the email) for 7 days. Our project is in the Frankfurt (Germany) region.
  • Cloudflare (R2 service): a second copy of the files, in the European Union.
  • Google (Gemini service): arranging courses into tabs, describing pictures in slides (when it is on, only when Anthropic is not available), reading podcasts aloud and checking their script, arranging documents from the group into your own material (when it is on), checking pictures sent in the track group, and in some cases Fred (see above).
  • OpenAI: building games and practice questions, summarizing lessons and topics, checking answers, writing podcasts, "Fred's notes" from the community, and in some cases Fred (see above).
  • Anthropic (Claude service): describing pictures in slides (when it is on), summarizing lessons and topics and checking answers when our server chooses it, and "Fred's notes" from the community when our server chooses it. See "Anthropic (Claude)".
  • Expo (650 Industries): app updates. Each time the app opens, it asks Expo's server whether there is a new version of the code. Along with the question go the network address (IP), a random ID of the installation (not linked to the phone or to the account, and replaced when you reinstall), the phone type, the app version and the channel. If the app crashed the previous time it opened, the error message of the crash is sent too. Expo may process the data in the United States and other places.
  • Sentry: crash reports, when they are on (see "What we collect, and why"). The reports are stored in the European Union, without the IP address.
  • Apple and Google: account sign-in. Apple also distributes the beta through TestFlight.

We give providers only what they need to provide their service. They process the data on our instructions and under their data processing terms, and must protect it at least as well as described on this page. We will disclose data to anyone else only if the law requires us to.

Other students: what you write in the community and your display name are visible to the group's members or to students of the same course. This is sharing you choose when you write there (see "The community"). What you share in the class library is visible to students of the same course too (see "The class library").

Where data is stored

  • The database, account sign-in, files and text are stored in the Frankfurt region, in the European Union.
  • Crash reports (when they are on) are stored at Sentry, in the European Union.
  • Supabase states that backups, logs, running server functions and its sub-processors may also process data outside the chosen region.
  • The tools Google runs (arranging courses, describing pictures in slides, reading podcasts aloud, arranging documents from the group, checking pictures in the track group, and maybe Fred) may be processed outside the European Union and outside Israel.
  • On iPhone, what is kept on your phone can enter your iCloud backup, according to your settings. We do not receive that backup. This includes the course structure from Moodle (names, tabs, links and deadlines), the text read from course files, the summaries, your practice and answers, your chats with Fred, Fred's notes from the group, reminders, your learning progress, the podcasts, the games, the names of files you shared, and your settings. The files themselves are not backed up: course files, files you shared, files and photos from the group, and the videos. If you do not want this in your backup, turn off the backup of Fred Study on your iPhone: Settings, your name, iCloud, Storage or Manage Account Storage, Backups, your device, then turn off Fred Study. On Android the app is not included in the Google backup.
  • The tools OpenAI runs (games, questions, summaries, answer checking, writing podcasts, "Fred's notes", and maybe Fred) may be processed in the United States and other places outside the European Union and outside Israel.
  • The tools Anthropic runs (describing pictures, summaries and answer checking when our server chooses it, and "Fred's notes" when our server chooses it) may be processed in the United States and other places outside the European Union and outside Israel.

How long data is kept

  • Account details, files, text and permissions: for as long as your account exists. You can delete it at any time.
  • Technical sign-in details (time, IP address, device type): at the sign-in service, for as long as your account exists.
  • The cloud provider's request logs (account ID, path, time and IP address of every request to the server, in all features, and at sign-in also the email): 7 days, also after the account is deleted, and then they are deleted automatically.
  • Crash reports (when they are on): at Sentry, 30 days.
  • Database backup: the cloud provider keeps a daily backup for 7 days. The files themselves are not part of this backup.
  • Your conversations with Fred: on your phone and not on our server, until you delete them or disconnect the account. Our server does not keep them.
  • Fred's memory (only if you agreed): on our server, until you delete it or the account; a temporary thing 7 days; a marker of an item you deleted, without the content, up to 30 days.
  • Podcast (when the service is on): the job's material and what was produced along the way: up to 24 hours from the start of the job, and up to 7 more days in the database backup. A finished episode: in your personal library in the cloud until you delete it or the account, and a copy on your phone until you delete it or disconnect the account.
  • What is sent to Gemini (including pictures from the group for the check): not kept by us; from a picture check only the result and the reason are kept, and a record of a picture blocked for its content is kept 30 days (see "Checking pictures in the track group"). At Google, for a limited period, as described above.
  • Shared summary (a summary of a lesson or topic, not linked to an account): up to 180 days from its last use. After that it is no longer handed out, and it is deleted later. After two reports of a mistake, the summary is deleted and written again.
  • What is sent to OpenAI: not kept by us. At OpenAI, up to 30 days (longer if the law requires), as described above.
  • What is sent to Anthropic: not kept by us. At Anthropic, up to 30 days, with the exceptions described above.
  • "Fred's notes": until 30 days after the end of the semester (if the end date of the semester is not known, the semester is taken to end 12 months after the course was created with us).
  • Messages and notes in the community: until you delete them or your account. The end of the semester does not delete them. Files and pictures in the group are deleted together with their message, or when the group is deleted, except a file of a reported message: it is kept for the reviewer until the decision, and at most 30 days. A copy that a group member opened or saved stays on their device.
  • The class library: an item you shared, until you remove it, until the team deletes the course, or until your account is deleted. An upload that did not finish: a few hours. A copy of a reported item: until the decision, and at most 30 days (see "The class library").
  • Display name, group memberships, blocks, and the record of terms and consent on the server: until your account is deleted. A block also until you remove it.
  • Track and year: until you leave the community or delete your account.
  • Your course list (membership in each course and the course name you sent): as long as the course is in your list. A course that left the list: access ends after 3 days, and its record (the course ID and when it last appeared, without access) stays until you leave the community or delete your account. A record of a course you left yourself: until your account is deleted.
  • Copy of a reported item: until the decision, or 30 days if there is no decision. The report itself: until the decision, and 90 more days after it.
  • An item kept hidden, and what a removed user wrote: 30 days after the decision.
  • Invite link: valid for 14 days, and deleted 7 days after it expires.
  • Technical records of the community: 7 days. The rate-limit record: 2 days.
  • A report of a mistake in Fred's answer (only if you confirmed sending it): the question, the answer and your words: 90 days (deleted by a daily cleaning, even when there is no new use), or until the account is deleted.
  • The count of reports ("Report a mistake" on a summary, "I think I was right" in practice): the count itself has no account ID: we do not keep who tapped. Kept without a time limit.
  • Messages to us by email: up to a year after the matter is closed.
  • Our server's log: 90 days, deleted by a daily cleaning, kept for operations and security. After your account is deleted, it is no longer linked to you.
  • The artificial-intelligence log (all the tools): the account ID (and for a podcast also a random episode ID and a "material tag": 32 characters computed from the account and from the material's identifier. It holds no content, and two students with the same file get different tags. It is used only for a monthly count and is deleted with the log after 30 days), time, type of action, cost, model and counts (never the content): 30 days. It is deleted by a daily cleaning, even when there is no new use. When you delete your account, the account ID is removed from the log, and the rows stay without an ID until they are deleted.

Deleting your account

No access to the app? Write to fredstudent03@gmail.com and state your account's email address (and if you hid your email at Apple, the address that ends with privaterelay.appleid.com). We send a confirmation email to the address on the account (Apple forwards it to you), and we delete the account only after you reply to us from that email. We send the confirmation email even if you wrote to us from your usual email, because a sender can be faked.

You can delete your account yourself, from inside the app: in the app's profile, under the account section, tap the option to delete your account. The app asks you to sign in again, the same way you signed in (Apple or Google). The deletion happens right away and cannot be undone.

What is deleted in the cloud:

  1. Your details, your file permissions, Fred's memory, reports on Fred's answers that you confirmed sending, and everything we received from Apple or Google when you signed in.
  2. Uploads that did not finish.
  3. Every file that no longer has any permission, together with its text.
  4. In the community: your display name, track and year, group memberships, the messages and notes you wrote (with their files and pictures), blocks (yours and those of people who blocked you), invite links you created, the items you shared in the class library (with their notes and files), the record of terms and consent, and your course list (course memberships, the course names you sent, and old fingerprints if any were sent).
  5. Your personal library in the cloud, with finished podcasts (when the service is on): the files are deleted from storage within about a quarter of an hour and are not in the backup, and the library record (title and date) stays in the database backup for up to 7 days.
  6. The account itself. Our server's log is no longer linked to you.

What is deleted on your phone: everything the app saved: courses, files, text, the course arrangement, your chats with Fred, podcast episodes, and your Reichman password if you saved it. Your sign-in to the Reichman website inside the sign-in window (cookies) stays, because the app cannot delete it. Removing the app from your phone deletes this sign-in. But on iPhone, deleting the app alone does not delete a Reichman password that was saved. Deleting your account from inside the app does delete it. If you want to remove the app without deleting your account, first press "Turn off and delete" (◊¥◊õ◊ë◊î ◊ï◊û◊ó◊ß◊¥) or "Disconnect account" (◊¥◊†◊™◊ß ◊ó◊©◊ë◊ï◊ü◊¥).

  • A file that another student also uploaded stays with that student. It is no longer linked to you.
  • If you signed in with Apple, we ask Apple to disconnect Fred Study from your Apple account. If that fails, the app explains how to remove it yourself in your phone's settings, under "Sign in with Apple".
  • If you signed in with Google, we delete everything we received from Google. You can also remove access on your Google account's permissions page: myaccount.google.com/permissions.
  • What was already sent to AI: deleting your account does not reach what was already sent to Google (Gemini) or OpenAI, including pictures from the group sent to Google for the check. They delete it on their own schedule: OpenAI within 30 days (longer if the law requires), Google after a limited period (see "Artificial intelligence").
  • Shared summary: deleting your account does not delete a shared summary, because it holds nothing that relates to you.
  • In the community: other people's replies to your messages are shown without the quote. A tag of you in someone else's message stays as a random ID that leads nowhere. A report you sent stays for review, not linked to you. A copy of a message of yours that was reported stays with the reviewer until the decision or 30 days, not linked to you. A file of such a message is kept for the reviewer the same way: until the decision, and at most 30 days. A file that a group member opened or saved stays on their device. "Fred's notes" stay, because they hold nothing that relates to you (see "Fred's notes from the community").
  • Backups: data deleted from the database may remain in the cloud provider's backup for up to 7 days, and is then deleted. We do not restore data of a deleted account from it.

Your rights

  • To see the data we hold about you.
  • To ask us to correct data that is wrong.
  • To ask us to delete your data and your account (also from inside the app, above).
  • To withdraw consent you gave. The way to do this is to stop using the app and delete your account, or to write to us. You can withdraw your consent to sending data to AI at any time, by turning each tool off in the app's profile. This stops new requests; it does not delete what was already sent. You can turn automatic refresh off at any time, and the saved password is deleted.

To use a right, write to fredstudent03@gmail.com. To make sure the request comes from you, we send a confirmation email to the address on the account (if you hid your email at Apple, Apple forwards it to you), and we handle the request only after you reply to us from that email, or we ask for another detail. We will respond as the law requires. If you are not satisfied with our answer, you can contact the Israeli Privacy Protection Authority.

Age

The app is meant for students aged 18 and over. Anyone under 18 should not use it. If we learn that an account was opened by someone under 18, we will delete it.

Security

  • All connections between the app, our server and our providers are encrypted (HTTPS).
  • According to our providers, their stored data is also encrypted.
  • Files are kept in a private storage area. Database access rules are enforced on the server, and a link to a file is created only for someone with permission and expires after a short time.
  • The number of actions per user is limited, to prevent abuse.
  • Your Reichman password is stored only on your phone. Without automatic refresh it is locked behind Face ID (on Android: a fingerprint). With automatic refresh, which you turned on yourself, it is stored without Face ID (see "Automatic refresh").

No system is completely secure. If a security incident affects your data, we will notify you and the authorities as the law requires.

Data from your Google account

When you sign in with Google, we ask only for the basic permissions, and receive your account ID, email, name and a link to your profile picture. They are stored at Supabase in Frankfurt. We use them only to identify you and manage your account. We do not access any other data in your Google account, do not use this data for advertising, and do not sell it or pass it to others.

Changes to this policy

  • This policy will be updated after a legal review, before the app opens to everyone.
  • Whenever we change it, we will update the date at the top of the page. If a change significantly affects how your data is used, we will let you know before it takes effect.
  • Before we use data for a new purpose, for example videos, we will update this page.

Contact

Questions, requests and inquiries about privacy: Jonathan Marco, fredstudent03@gmail.com
